> Yet ride he did, plunging into combat against invading battledroids on Coruscant. Kybucks, native to Kashyyyk, are known for their amazing speed and agility. Such traits are vital for survival on the Wookiee homeworld. More than a few predators have failed to catch the kybuck, losing the chase in a quick, near-impossible, vectoraltering jumping turn or flat-out run. In the Star Wars Miniatures Game, combining the Force-wielding Yoda with the speedy Kybuck makes for a lethal combination for enemy squads. Galloping Attack enables the unit to make the most of Yoda's devastating attack bonus. Yoda can attack multiple enemies in a single turn and have enough movement left over to escape immediate danger or seek cover. If a particularly opportune series of targets presents itself, Yoda can boost his movement even more through the use of Master Speed -- up to five times in a single game, if he forgoes using Lightsaber Block. [script removed][script removed] Comparing this unit with previous incarnations of Yoda in the Star Wars Miniatures Game, this unit costs the least, with the exception of Yoda of Dagobah. It has the most Hit Points and a Defense comparable to most of the others. He has the potential for the most attacks, thanks to Galloping Attack, though Double Attack is in line with previous versions.
— Source: Web Articles.pdf [Star Wars Roleplaying Game Saga Edition (2007)] (scan p; Yoda. POV: Yoda.

> Because things didn't work, you had to make them work and improvise every time. [Director François] Truffaut said it better than I could — something like, ‘You start a film and you want to make the greatest one ever made. Halfway through, you just want to finish the damned thing.’ That's the way I felt. Halfway through, my crew was falling apart. Many of the people left; they were ill. So, no, I never stopped and said, ‘Boy, oh boy, have we made a terrific film.’ ” 468 The production may have been more complicated than Lucas originally anticipated; Kershner explains the madness of Empire Strikes Back: “George also came over once when I was shooting the X-wing being pulled out of the water and moving across the swamp, based on the magic power of our little man. It had taken some time to set up, a few hours actually, and now we did the shot, and the haze was right— because we had the set closed off so that you actually had clouds hanging—and then the ship came out of the water. It looked beautiful, and there was moss and seaweed, and the water is dripping off, and suddenly the two wings just collapsed. I felt so badly for George, because I knew it was his money. I said, ‘What happened?’
— Source: Star Wars Insider #49 (2000); Yoda; Luke Skywalker — entry/section: Kershner interview, printed p. 29.
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from novels tmp lucas bts kaminski secret history (text copy `novels_tmp_lucas_bts_kaminski_secret_history_pdf_45a5e53edc`).)

## Star Wars Insider #55 (2001)

> I usually describe Mace as a voice of reason. He thinks before he speaks. He's pretty calculating, mostly even-tempered. And he's not to be trifled with. He knows that the war is coming. I think he has a pretty good idea of who is behind it, but nobody is willing to just step up right now and say exactly who the bad guy is. Everyone is being very cautious. And they're starting to get pretty aggressive about what they need to do to participate in it, or to nip this thing in the bud. He seems to be the second-most-powerful Jedi on that Council, next to Yoda.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #55 (2001).
Note (our notes, around the quote): Mace Windu; Yoda — entry/section: Samuel L. Jackson describing Mace Windu (verified on crop): ""I usually describe Mace as a voice of reason. And they're starting to get pretty aggressive about what they need to do to participate in it, or to nip this thing in the bud. > I guess they have been in service with each other for a very long time. He's also a student. Yoda is definitely Master, even though Mace is referred to as Master himself. Everyone has a secondary position to Yoda, in some way. We're all under him. We aren't as intuitive, we aren't as strong mentally, and we aren't as strong spiritually. Yoda is incorruptible. He's kind of unfathomable in a lot of ways. But spiritually, Mace and Yoda are kind of in tune. That's why they can talk to each other, and a lot of times almost finish each other's sentences in an interesting kind of way. But Yoda is definitely the stronger and smarter and more spiritual of the two.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #55 (2001).
Note (our notes, around the quote): Mace Windu; Yoda — entry/section: Samuel L. > I know it was a challenge for Frank to characterize something so old and green. I was very flattered when he asked me how I came up with C-3PO's voice. He was searching for a voice for Yoda at the time, as was George. Of course, being asked for advice by such an accomplished performer hushed me to incoherence. But we talked about believing in the characters we played and their own personalities rather than ours. I don't think I helped much but was delighted when they realized that everything about his voice and his timing was the character.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #55 (2001).
Note (our notes, around the quote): C-3PO; Yoda — entry/section: Anthony Daniels on Frank Oz asking how he found C-3PO's voice while Oz was searching for Yoda's (verified on crop): ""I know it was a challenge for Frank to characterize something so old and green. Of course, being asked for advice by such an accomplished performer hushed me to incoherence. But we talked about believing in the characters we played and their own personalities rather than ours. > So it seems the Jedi who are left are either working on the dark side or are Obi-Wan Kenobi and Yoda. I suppose something happens, and they split. I believe I go to look over Luke, because I take him somewhere when he's a baby, and Princess Leia gets taken somewhere else when she's a baby. I'm living near where Luke lives in Episode IV, so I must be watching over him, and living in a small white room in a hut. Obviously, there's a huge trust between me and Anakin in these films, which probably leads to the ultimate betrayal in the end.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #60 (2002).
Note (our notes, around the quote): Obi-Wan Kenobi; Yoda; Luke Skywalker; Leia Organa Solo; Darth Vader (Anakin Skywalker) — entry/section: Ewan McGregor on Obi-Wan's future: ""So it seems the Jedi who are left are either working on the dark side or are Obi-Wan Kenobi and Yoda. I suppose something happens, and they split. I believe I go to look over Luke, because I take him somewhere when he's a baby, and Princess Leia gets taken somewhere else when she's a baby. I'm living near where Luke lives in Episode IV, so I must be watching over him, and living in a small white room in a hut. Obviously, there's a huge trust between me and Anakin in these films, which probably leads to the ultimate betrayal in the end." —Ewan McGregor, Star Wars Insider 60, p. 30" Source: *Star Wars Insider #60* (2002), scan p.30

Jackson; Count Dooku as an old gentleman rather than a monster; Darth Sidious needing an already-trained apprentice. > In the last one,
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> they built a new puppet with a different kind of material, and it gave him a slightly different nature. He was younger, so we rationalized that we could get away with that. Now, we're trying to bridge that particular version of Yoda with The Empire Strikes Back Yoda. I think we're doing it. We've got a middle ground between the two of them, and he still looks and acts like Yoda to where I don't think you're going to say it's not the same character.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Star Wars Insider #60 (2002).
Note (our notes, around the quote): Yoda — entry/section: George Lucas on the digital Yoda: "Getting Yoda right was crucial, as Lucas acknowledged that the Yoda of The Phantom Menace wasn't exactly perfect. "In the last one," he said, "they built a new puppet with a different kind of material, and it gave him a slightly different nature. He was younger, so we rationalized that we could get away with that. Now, we're trying to bridge that particular version of Yoda with The Empire Strikes Back Yoda. I think we're doing it. We've got a middle ground between the two of them, and he still looks and acts like Yoda to where I don't think you're going to say it's not the same character." —George Lucas, Star Wars Insider 60, p. 59" Source: *Star Wars Insider #60* (2002), scan p.59
![Quote image: *Star Wars Insider #60* (2002), scan p.59 — full quote as printed](quotes/ins060-s59-getting-yoda-right-was-crucial-as.jpg)
Our notes: `notes/materials/038-star-wars-insider/060-ins060.md:436`

> Yoda's astonishing moves in his epic duel against Count Dooku may be revealed on close examination to be a masterful demonstration of the standard components of Form IV. Yoda's moves flow from one to another in the smooth transitions characteristic of this Form. Three kinds of rotation, or su ma, figure prominently in Yoda's style. They are jung su ma (spinning), ton su ma (somersaults), and en su ma (cartwheels). These three moves represent the three possible axes of rotation in three-dimensional space. Together with flying Force-enhanced jumps, the rotational su ma moves compose most of Yoda's lightsaber fighting technique, making him the ultimate example of Form IV.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #62 (2002).
Note (our notes, around the quote): Yoda; Count Dooku — entry/section: Lightsaber forms: Yoda and Form IV: "Yoda's astonishing moves in his epic duel against Count Dooku may be revealed on close examination to be a masterful demonstration of the standard components of Form IV. Yoda's moves flow from one to another in the smooth transitions characteristic of this Form. Three kinds of rotation, or su ma, figure prominently in Yoda's style. They are jung su ma (spinning), ton su ma (somersaults), and en su ma (cartwheels). These three moves represent the three possible axes of rotation in three-dimensional space. Together with flying Force-enhanced jumps, the rotational su ma moves compose most of Yoda's lightsaber fighting technique, making him the ultimate example of Form IV." Source: *Star Wars Insider #62* (2002), scan p.37

> Man, it takes a lot to impress kids today. Yoda lifts a multi-ton stone and metal pillar, and people are peeved that he breaks a sweat. Seriously, though, it does take effort to lift heavy masses by using the Force. It's not the same kind of effort required to stave off lower back pain when you're hefting furniture around, but rather a kind of meditative, internal cosmic kind of deal called concentration.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #64 (2002).

## Star Wars Insider #70 (2003)

> The one where Obi-Wan Kenobi fights his main bad guy, [Durge]. I kind of like how that fight turned out because it's fast and brutal. I also love the two Mace episodes. He's on his own, beating the heck out of tons of droids in pure Jedi style and that was a lot of fun. We don't really get to see Mace do too much in the movie—just a little bit at the end of Episode II—and he's supposed to be second to Yoda, so it was our chance to really flex Mace's muscles.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #70 (2003).

> One of his first changes was to “Remove probe zaps snow squirrel,” while others were: “Perhaps Luke cuts wampa arm off … Search outtakes of Yoda throwing can … Double-print entire sequence [of dark side tree] … Play some of Yoda line, ‘You will destroy all that they have worked for’ over Luke … Add new wide shot Cloud City before Leia in VistaVision plate … Remove Vader line, ‘You have less power than you think’ … CS Vader with fist, ‘If you only knew the power of the dark side’ … Vader hollers, ‘Luke!’ when Luke falls … Extend head of Chewie growl to play over off-screen Luke line, ‘May the Force be with you.’ ”
>
> “What’s the screen direction of the object flying past you; was it coming toward you or away from you?” Lucas says. “Empire is a completely cinematic, editorial way of making a movie, which is where I come from.

> One day I just kinda asked George if I could have a different-colored lightsaber. He asked me what color I wanted, so I said purple. He then asked me why. I said, 'Well, I just want a different color because I am like high up on the Jedi Council and have a bit more power, second to Yoda, and I think I should have a different color.' George said there are only two colors, but that he would think about it. When I came back later, he said he had something to show me, and there it was.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #80 (2005); Mace Windu — entry/section: The purple lightsaber.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Mace Windu — entry/section: The purple lightsaber: ""One day I just kinda asked George if I could have a different-colored lightsaber. He asked me what color I wanted, so I said purple. He then asked me why. I said, 'Well, I just want a different color because I am like high up on the Jedi Council and have a bit more power, second to Yoda, and I think I should have a different color.' George said there are only two colors, but that he would think about it. When I came back later, he said he had something to show me, and there it was." —Samuel L. Jackson, Star Wars Insider 80, pp. 43–44" Source: *Star Wars Insider #80* (2005), scan p.43

## Star Wars Insider #90 (2006)

> This is what would have been one of these little buildings, but we built it on a separate scale, because this is the model where the camera is inside. Yoda's in there, Chewbacca is in there. It's [used] when Order 66 goes down and the clone troopers try to kill Yoda, just before he escapes Kashyyyk, so all of that that is going on inside this [model].
— Source: Star Wars Insider #90 (2006); Yoda; Chewbacca — entry/section: Gernand on the Yoda pagoda.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Yoda; Chewbacca — entry/section: Gernand on the Yoda pagoda: ""This is what would have been one of these little buildings, but we built it on a separate scale, because this is the model where the camera is inside. It's [used] when Order 66 goes down and the clone troopers try to kill Yoda, just before he escapes Kashyyyk, so all of that that is going on inside this [model]." —Brian Gernand, Star Wars Insider 90, p. 35" Source: *Star Wars Insider #90* (2006), scan p.35

— Source: Star Wars Insider #95 (2007); Yoda — entry/section: Nightmare.

There is no try" Master Yoda; Yoda photo; Phil Tippett photo; Features: 16 The Clone Wars; 20 The Big Interview (Stuart Freeborn); 29 Nick Dudman; 36 Cantina Creatures; 40 Top Ten Toughest Ever Creatures (wampa art); 53 Effects Maestros (hand-read)
> Still, if we thought something was so strained as to be camp, we would adopt it ourselves. We found many uses for the line “Many Bothans died to bring you this information.” I still like the sentiment in “Do. Or do not. There is no try,” because that comes directly out of George’s personality. He had spent his entire early career with people, sometimes important people, telling him, “You can’t do that.” Henry Ford had said it before, in a slightly different way: “Whether you think you can, or you think you can’t—you’re right.” I was the recipient of some of this Lucas wisdom one day when I wasn’t expecting it.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #102 (2008).
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Inside the Star Wars Empire (Bill Kimberlin) (text copy `novels_tmp_lucas_bts_kimberlin_inside_the_star_wars_empire_epub_0d4920d9fb`).)
(Full sentence/passage taken from the source text on file: `novels_tmp_lucas_bts_kimberlin_inside_the_star_wars_empire_epub`.)

## Star Wars Insider #104 (2008)

> The Clone Wars has all of the characters everybody knows—from Yoda to Anakin to Mace Windu to Obi-Wan—they're all there. The live-action series, meanwhile, has nobody [you'll know], because it's after Episode III, so everybody's dead, basically, or hiding somewhere. You hear about the Emperor, just like you do in Episode IV, but it's mostly about a whole different world. I mean, there are a million stories in the big city—you've only seen one of them.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #104 (2008); none — entry/section: Lucas on the live-action series.
Note (our notes, around the quote): none — entry/section: Lucas on the live-action series: ""The Clone Wars has all of the characters everybody knows—from Yoda to Anakin to Mace Windu to Obi-Wan—they're all there. The live-action series, meanwhile, has nobody [you'll know], because it's after Episode III, so everybody's dead, basically, or hiding somewhere. You hear about the Emperor, just like you do in Episode IV, but it's mostly about a whole different world. I mean, there are a million stories in the big city—you've only seen one of them." —George Lucas, Star Wars Insider 104, p. 8" Source: *Star Wars Insider #104* (2008), scan p.8

> Acting is acting, whether it is a human actor, a CG character, or a puppet. It's all the same. Most people think of Yoda as being real, because he is the height of puppet artistry. After Frank Oz did Empire, I tried to get him nominated for an Academy Award, but we heard back that puppetry wasn't an art. I think it's an art—and Yoda represented the highest level of that art.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #109 (2009); Yoda — entry/section: Lucas: Yoda puppetry.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Yoda — entry/section: Lucas: Yoda puppetry: ""Acting is acting, whether it is a human actor, a CG character, or a puppet. It's all the same. After Frank Oz did Empire, I tried to get him nominated for an Academy Award, but we heard back that puppetry wasn't an art. I think it's an art—and Yoda represented the highest level of that art." —George Lucas (The Making of The Phantom Menace, Del Rey, 1999), Star Wars Insider 109, p. 73" Source: *Star Wars Insider #109* (2009), scan p.73

> Luke hesitated; but however the Imperials were getting information out of the New Republic’s inner sanctum, Winter was certainly far above suspicion. “The problem is that if this alleged Jedi out on Jomark really is Jorus C’baoth, he has to be over a hundred by now. I know there are some species that live longer than that, but he’s supposed to be human.”
>
> Luke thought about that. It was possible, he knew. Yoda had certainly had a long life—a good nine hundred years—and as a general rule, smaller species usually had shorter life spans than larger ones. But usually didn’t mean always; and after many hours of records searches, Luke still hadn’t figured out just what species Yoda had belonged to. Perhaps a better approach might be to try to find out how long the Emperor had lived.
— Source: Dark Force Rising — notes part 2: ch. 3–5; Luke: if Jomark's Jedi is C'baoth.

> Luke hurried to catch up. It was, he knew, something of a long shot: even if the spot he was looking for was within Artoo’s sensor range, there was no guarantee that the droid would be able to distinguish healthy alien plants from unhealthy ones. But it was worth a try. Yoda, he had long suspected, had managed to stay hidden from the Emperor and Vader only because the dark side cave near his home had somehow shielded his own influence on the Force. For C’baoth to have remained unnoticed, it followed that Jomark must also have a similar focus of dark side power somewhere.

> Up close, the tree straddling the cave looked as evil as he remembered it: twisted, dark, and vaguely brooding, as if it was itself alive with the dark side of the Force. Perhaps it was. Luke couldn’t tell for sure, not with the overwhelming emanations of the cave flooding his senses. It was, clearly, the source of the low-level pressure he’d felt ever since his arrival on Dagobah, and for a moment he wondered why the effect had never been this strong before.
>
> Perhaps because Yoda had always been here before, his presence shielding Luke from the true strength of the cave’s power.
>
> But now Yoda was gone … and Luke was facing the cave alone.

>

TOYDARIAN TROUBLE: "Some species are naturally immune to the Jedi mind tricks of all but the most powerful Masters. Anakin = "prophesied individual who will restore balance and harmony to the Force"; Council incl. Yoda "sense danger in the boy". Qui-Gon became Master training first Padawan to Knighthood; "his second apprentice failed to become a Knight. Obi-Wan is Qui-Gon's third Padawan". Tatooine duel: "On Tatooine, Qui-Gon battles a Sith warrior wielding a deadly lightsabre. Since lightsabres are seldom handled by non-Jedi, the order primarily uses them as defence against blaster bolts rather than other lightsabres. However, lightsabre duelling forms part of Jedi training." Jedi field gear (grappling spike launcher, liquid cable). DATA FILE: "The initiates' ability to do this [construct lightsabres] successfully proves their developed sensitivity to the Force." Qui-Gon "motivated to take a more active role than that traditionally taken by the Jedi."

> References: A&EG Arms & Equipment Guide CCW Coruscant & the Core Worlds GCG Galactic Campaign Guide DSSB Dark Side Sourcebook HG Hero’s Guide PotJ Power of the Jedi Sourcebook UAA Ultimate Alien Anthology UAd Ultimate Adversaries COLLECTION 3 REVENGE OF THE SITH COLLECTION Chapter One: Heroes Obi-Wan Kenobi General Kenobi, now a member of the Jedi Council, and his former Padawan, Anakin Skywalker, returned from the Outer Rim sieges to rescue Palpatine from General Grievous and Count Dooku. Later sent to hunt for Grievous on Utapau, Kenobi was unable to provide guidance to Anakin in his darkest hour—and, after surviving his clone troopers’ assassination attempt, he learned the terrible truth behind the massacre at the Jedi Temple. On Mustafar, the Master and his former student would cross blades—but not for the last time. Yoda Obi-Wan Kenobi: Male Human Jedi guardian 7/Jedi Master 5/Jedi investigator 2; Init +3 (+3 Dex); Defense 24 (+3 Dex, +11 class), 30 with Master Defense; Spd 10 m; VP/WP 119/14; Atk +17/+12/+7 melee* (5d8+3/18–20, lightsaber) or +16/+11/+6 ranged (by weapon); SQ contact, deflect (attack –3, defense +4, extend defense and attack), force secret (Affect Mind +1, Force Defense +1), target bonus +1; SV Fort +12, Ref +13, Will +12; SZ M; FP 9; Rep +4; Str 15, Dex 16, Con 14, Int 13, Wis 14, Cha 13. Over the past eight centuries, Yoda had guided hundreds of Jedi to knighthood. Over the past three years, he guided the order through the Clone Wars. In the last days of the Republic, he felt a terrible tremor in the Force as his fellow Jedi were betrayed by the treacherous clone troopers.
>
> has placed too much trust in the prophesized ability of the Chosen One
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> He concludes that Mace and the others failed to defeat Darth Sidious.
— Source: Star Wars Revenge of the Sith: The Visual Dictionary (DK, 2005): hand-read notes; p34 Yoda [KEY]: arrived at the Temple.

> THE SITH HAVE WAITED a millennium for the birth of one who is powerful enough to return them from hiding. Darth Sidious is that one—the Sith's revenge on the Jedi order for having nearly eradicated the practitioners of the dark side of the Force. Trained by Darth Plagueis, Sidious, in his guise as Palpatine, understood that the corrupt Republic and the subservient Jedi Order could be brought down by playing to the weaknesses of the former: its mindless bureaucracy and attachment to power.
>
> Sidious does not consider himself evil but rather a savior.
>
> Sidious has one more task to perform before his conquest is complete: to kill Yoda. Then the light side of the Force will be eclipsed, and the Jedi order will cease to exist.

— Source: Star Wars: The Ultimate Visual Guide (DK, 2005): A Galaxy Far, Far Away and The Old Republic (cover to p. 49), hand-read notes; p18-19 The Jedi Order [KEY]. POV: Great Holocron; OBJECT MOVEMENT; JEDI SPIRITS; Dark Woman; about Yoda.
Note (our notes, around the quote): p18-19 The Jedi Order [KEY]: "Founded as a philosophical study group ... For 25,000 years, the Jedi served as peacemakers of the Galactic Republic"; initiates "rarely older than six months when they began their training". Will of the Force: "Analyzing Anakin Skywalker's blood, Obi-Wan Kenobi discovered a count even higher than Master Yoda's." Finding potential Jedi ("regarded the Jedi as baby snatchers"). Lightsaber training (safety-blade generators, helmets, remotes). Archives "the greatest library in the Galactic Republic". Great Holocron: "The largest and most powerful Holocron is the dodecahedral Great Holocron". OBJECT MOVEMENT: "In his duel with Count Dooku on Geonosis, Yoda (left) used the Force to halt the fall of heavy stones ... Jedi utilize this talent not only to push, pull, and lift objects, but also to redirect projectiles and guide their starships through combat." Mind tricks: "Aliens with highly organized mental facilities [sic], such as Hutts and Toydarians, are naturally immune to Jedi mind tricks." JEDI SPIRITS: "The Sith Lord Darth Plagueis discovered it was possible to merge with the Force and still retain individual consciousness, but was not interested in the nonmaterial world. Jedi Master Qui-Gon Jinn succeeded, and later returned from the netherworld to pass his knowledge on to Yoda and Obi-Wan Kenobi". Dark Woman: original name "An'ya Kuro"; discovered "four-year-old Ki-Adi-Mundi".

A Night at the Opera: "The Tragedy of Darth Plagueis the Wise, the story of a Sith Lord who had the power to keep people safe from death." Battle on Utapau: "After a ferocious lightsaber duel and high-speed chase ... the Jedi finally destroys Grievous using the cyborg's own blaster." SHOWDOWN ON CORUSCANT: "Palpatine surprises them by igniting his own lightsaber, quickly slaying all but Mace Windu, who learns too late that his opponent's greatest weapon is Anakin Skywalker." Caption: "Palpatine uses Sith lightning to attack Mace Windu, but the Jedi Master uses his lightsaber to force the lethal energy bolts back upon the Sith Lord, deforming his features. Palpatine summons aid from Anakin, who betrays Mace Windu, disarming the Jedi and, unintentionally, allowing Palpatine to kill Windu." A New Name: Vader's first assignment to eliminate all Jedi at the Temple. SITH RITUALS: "Chancellor Palpatine spent many years studying ancient Holocrons to learn the secrets of the Sith. The Holocrons enabled him to channel Sith spirits (right), who taught him how to harness dark-side energy and release lethal bolts of lightning. During the Clone Wars, Palpatine conducted Sith rituals on Coruscant that radiated unnerving ripples in the Force". Defending Kashyyyk: Yoda's departure "part of a plan to lure Darth Sidious into the open".

> “I do, yes, I do!” Yoda said. “Sick have I become. Old and weak.” Pointing a crooked finger at his guest, he added, “When nine hundred years old you reach, look as good you will not. Earned it, I have.” He was so weak, he could barely manage to pull his blanket up over himself.

## Star Wars: Obsession #1–5 (Dark Horse, Nov 2004 – Apr 2005)

> And the whole town is run from here, by a Black Sun killer named Xist.
>
> Aayla, I don't care about Black Sun...
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> I know your mission, Obi-Wan. Xist is providing weapons -- and intel -- to the Confederacy. He'll be able to tell you whatever you need to know about the enemy's movements.
>
> Obi-Wan... if I told you that he's incredibly well-defended and that his skills with a vibrowhip rival yours with a lightsaber -- would you reconsider allowing me to fight at your side?
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> No. Master Yoda made it clear that my mission could not endanger yours.
>
> I appreciate your help thus far, but I'm on my own here.
>
> Then at least call in Anakin. I know that he's been allowed leave as well--
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> Oh, I'm quite certain he'd rather not be disturbed...
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Star Wars: Obsession #1 (Dark Horse, November 2004): hand-read notes; p13: Aayla.
Note (our notes, around the quote): p13: Aayla: "And the whole town is run from here, by a Black Sun killer named Xist." Obi-Wan: "Aayla, I don't care about Black Sun..." Aayla: "I know your mission, Obi-Wan. Xist is providing weapons -- and intel -- to the Confederacy. He'll be able to tell you whatever you need to know about the enemy's movements." "Obi-Wan... if I told you that he's incredibly well-defended and that his skills with a vibrowhip rival yours with a lightsaber -- would you reconsider allowing me to fight at your side?" Obi-Wan: "No. Master Yoda made it clear that my mission could not endanger yours." "I appreciate your help thus far, but I'm on my own here." Aayla: "Then at least call in Anakin. I know that he's been allowed leave as well--" Obi-Wan: "Oh, I'm quite certain he'd rather not be disturbed..."

> We can't spare any additional resources. Nearly every available Jedi is being diverted to the Outer Rim. You and Anakin have been allowed a brief respite only because you've been on the front lines for nearly a year -- but even you'll be needed soon. I suggest you rest.
>
> And yet committed to your search, you remain, Obi-Wan?
>
> Yes, Master Yoda. She's still alive, I can sense it... and I can bring her to justice. If I don't act, she could destroy entire armies -- wipe out entire worlds...
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> We're well aware of the threat posed by Ventress. You will be permitted to continue your mission. But, be careful, Obi-Wan...
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Star Wars: Obsession #1 (Dark Horse, November 2004): hand-read notes; p23 [KEY]: Coruscant, Council (holograms). Mace.
Note (our notes, around the quote): p23 [KEY]: Coruscant, Council (holograms). Mace: "We can't spare any additional resources. Nearly every available Jedi is being diverted to the Outer Rim. You and Anakin have been allowed a brief respite only because you've been on the front lines for nearly a year -- but even you'll be needed soon. I suggest you rest." Yoda: "And yet committed to your search, you remain, Obi-Wan?" Obi-Wan (holo): "Yes, Master Yoda. She's still alive, I can sense it... and I can bring her to justice. If I don't act, she could destroy entire armies -- wipe out entire worlds..." Mace: "We're well aware of the threat posed by Ventress. You will be permitted to continue your mission. But, be careful, Obi-Wan..."
Our notes: `notes/materials/049-obsession/ob-01.md:29`

> This is but a small quest in a life filled with important journeys. May the Force be with you.
>
> You didn't tell them about your condition. You can barely stand.
>
> I'll heal. But, you were right about one thing... I can't do this alone.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> But you've already done enough, Aayla. Thank you for the loan of your starfighter.
>
> Don't mention it. Will you now ask Anakin for help?
>
> Yes, although it won't be easy to convince him. He still believes that he killed Ventress on Coruscant.
>
> I'm sure you'll win him over. You are his Master.
>
> Now that he's a Jedi Knight, I hope he still thinks so...
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Star Wars: Obsession #1 (Dark Horse, November 2004): hand-read notes; p24: Yoda (holo).
Note (our notes, around the quote): p24: Yoda (holo): "This is but a small quest in a life filled with important journeys. May the Force be with you." Thank you for the loan of your starfighter." "Don't mention it. Will you now ask Anakin for help?" "Yes, although it won't be easy to convince him. He still believes that he killed Ventress on Coruscant." Aayla: "I'm sure you'll win him over. You are his Master." Obi-Wan (caption): "Now that he's a Jedi Knight, I hope he still thinks so..." Our notes: `notes/materials/049-obsession/ob-01.md:30`

> The Confederacy has pulled back to the Outer Rim. Republic forces have started a series of sieges against several key worlds. Alderaan has dealings with a number of systems there -- and I need to view the situation first-hand.
>
> Purposefully going into a war zone, Senator --?
>
> I will accept full responsibility for any consequences, Chancellor. I will have a cadre of my own troops accompanying me -- but I need an experienced Jedi General at my side. Preferably, someone familiar with the systems.
>
> If you insist on going, General Kenobi has extensive experience in the Outer Rim. He'll need young Skywalker's aid, of course.
>
> I'm afraid that's impossible. Anakin is on leave and Obi-Wan is tracking down Commander Ventress.
— Source: Star Wars: Obsession #2 (Dark Horse, December 2004): hand-read notes.
Note (our notes, around the quote): p9: Coruscant, Palpatine's office (Mace, Yoda, red guards, Mas Amedda). Bail Organa (unnamed in dialogue; Alderaan senator, green tunic): "The Confederacy has pulled back to the Outer Rim. Republic forces have started a series of sieges against several key worlds. Alderaan has dealings with a number of systems there -- and I need to view the situation first-hand." Palpatine: "Purposefully going into a war zone, Senator --?" Bail: "I will accept full responsibility for any consequences, Chancellor. I will have a cadre of my own troops accompanying me -- but I need an experienced Jedi General at my side. Preferably, someone familiar with the systems." Palpatine: "If you insist on going, General Kenobi has extensive experience in the Outer Rim. He'll need young Skywalker's aid, of course." Mace: "I'm afraid that's impossible. Anakin is on leave and Obi-Wan is tracking down Commander Ventress."
Our notes: `notes/materials/049-obsession/ob-02.md:15`

> Really? I thought the Jedi Order believed her dead?
>
> Not yet known is the truth.
>
> Then Obi-Wan should be ordered to the Outer Rim. There really is no --
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> I'll go. I'm sure no one here doubts that I can be at least as effective as Master Kenobi?
>
> Of course not, Master Windu. We're all confident that you'll lead us to victory.
>
> And may the Force be with you.
— Source: Star Wars: Obsession #2 (Dark Horse, December 2004): hand-read notes; p10 [KEY]: Palpatine.
